What brings you in to see me?\n\nPatient: I feel like my feelings and motivation have dried up; it’s getting harder to connect with my clients or even with my own life. I just feel completely shut down inside, and because it's really starting to affect my ability to function at work, I agreed to come in for this evaluation.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently had any unusual experiences, such as hearing voices when no one is around, or feeling like others might be controlling or reading your thoughts?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear any voices, but I have noticed something else unusual over the last couple of months. I've started feeling like there is a coordinated effort at my work and my aunt's hospital, where people are dropping hints in emails and meetings that they're secretly evaluating or investigating me for some kind of program.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling shut down and noticing these signs at work, have you been experiencing a persistently sad or down mood, or any changes in your sleep and appetite?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed feeling persistently sad or down—it's more that I just feel very blank, flat, and empty inside. My mind just feels more quiet and blank rather than jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Since you started feeling empty and unmotivated, have you found yourself withdrawing from friends and family, or having a hard time keeping up with daily chores and personal care?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been withdrawing a lot lately—canceling plans with friends and ignoring their calls so I can just spend my evenings alone. I've also really let things slide at home, and basic chores like grocery shopping and cleaning are piling up because I feel too heavy and unmotivated to handle them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts, feelings, or body movements are not your own, or that they are being directly controlled or influenced by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like an outside force is controlling my mind or body. It doesn't feel like anyone else is doing it to me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you sometimes freeze up in the middle of a hallway. Can you describe what happens during these moments, how long they last, and if you are able to move or speak?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm usually not even aware it's happening until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it. My coworkers tell me I'll just stand in the hallway or copy room staring at nothing for several minutes, holding a file. Because of that, I don't really know if my muscles are stiff or if I look awkward during those times.\n\nDoctor: How do you believe these people are collecting information about you, and what do you think they plan to do with the results of this investigation?"

I've also really let things slide at home, and basic chores like grocery shopping and cleaning are piling up because I feel too heavy and unmotivated to handle them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts, feelings, or body movements are not your own, or that they are being directly controlled or influenced by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like an outside force is controlling my mind or body. Even when I suddenly freeze up in the middle of a hallway or feel completely blank, it just feels like my own system is shutting down and getting stuck. It doesn't feel like anyone else is doing it to me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you sometimes freeze up in the middle of a hallway. Can you describe what happens during these moments, how long they last, and if you are able to move or speak?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm usually not even aware it's happening until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it. My coworkers tell me I'll just stand in the hallway or copy room staring at nothing for several minutes, holding a file. During those moments, I don't register the passage of time and I can't seem to move or speak at all.\n\nDoctor: When you have those moments of freezing up, do your muscles feel very stiff, or has anyone told you that you hold unusual or awkward positions during those times?\n\nPatient: My coworkers have told me that I'll suddenly stop in the hallway or copy room, staring at nothing while holding a file. I don't actually realize I'm frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it, so I'm not sure how my muscles feel. Because of that, I don't really know if my muscles are stiff or if I look awkward during those times.\n\nDoctor: How do you believe these people are collecting information about you, and what do you think they plan to do with the results of this investigation?\n\nPatient: I feel like they are collecting information through subtle hints dropped in my work emails and during our staff meetings. As for my sleep and appetite, I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed any specific changes there.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this emptiness and lack of motivation, and did these feelings start before or after you began noticing the signs of being investigated?\n\nPatient: The empty, shut-down feelings and the lack of motivation started first, about eight months ago when my aunt's health got worse and my caseload picked up. The feeling that people at work and the hospital are secretly investigating me is much more recent, only starting about two months ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you or those close to you noticed any difficulties with your thinking, such as your thoughts feeling tangled, or finding it hard to express yourself clearly when speaking?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed my thoughts feeling tangled or had trouble expressing myself clearly. My mind just feels more quiet and blank rather than jumbled.\n\nDoctor: Since you started feeling empty and unmotivated, have you found yourself withdrawing from friends and family, or having a hard time keeping up with daily chores and personal care?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been withdrawing a lot lately—canceling plans with friends and ignoring their calls so I can just spend my evenings alone. I've also really let things slide at home, and basic chores like grocery shopping and cleaning are piling up because I feel too heavy and unmotivated to handle them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts, feelings, or body movements are not your own, or that they are being directly controlled or influenced by an outside force?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt like an outside force is controlling my mind or body. Even when I suddenly freeze up in the middle of a hallway or feel completely blank, it just feels like my own system is shutting down and getting stuck. It doesn't feel like anyone else is doing it to me.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you sometimes freeze up in the middle of a hallway. Can you describe what happens during these moments, how long they last, and if you are able to move or speak?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm usually not even aware it's happening until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it. My coworkers tell me I'll just stand in the hallway or copy room staring at nothing for several minutes, holding a file. During those moments, I don't register the passage of time and I can't seem to move or speak at all.\n\nDoctor: When you have those moments of freezing up, do your muscles feel very stiff, or has anyone told you that you hold unusual or awkward positions during those times?\n\nPatient: My coworkers have told me that I'll suddenly stop in the hallway or copy room, staring at nothing while holding a file. I don't actually realize I'm frozen until someone touches my arm or calls my name to snap me out of it, so I'm not sure how my muscles feel. Because of that, I don't really know if my muscles are stiff or if I look awkward during those times.\n\nDoctor: How do you believe these people are collecting information about you, and what do you think they plan to do with the results of this investigation?\n\nPatient: I feel like they are collecting information through subtle hints dropped in my work emails and during our staff meetings. I believe it's all for some kind of secret program or investigation, but I'm not completely sure yet what they plan to do with the results, which only adds to my anxiety.\n\nDoctor: To help me understand everything fully, are you currently taking any medications, or do you regularly use alcohol or recreational substances?\n\nPatient: No, I am not currently taking any medications. I also don't use alcohol or any recreational substances."

Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings). Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
